oxc-sourcemap commit e533e6ca4d08c538d8d4df74eacd29437851591f:
https://github.com/oxc-project/oxc-sourcemap/blob/e533e6ca4d08c538d8d4df74eacd29437851591f/src/encode.rs#L331

That source credits serde_json commit d12e943590208da738c092db92c34b39796a2538:
https://github.com/serde-rs/json/blob/d12e943590208da738c092db92c34b39796a2538/src/ser.rs#L2079

for benchmarks. These benchmark files are not included in the published crate.

### Text 0a0e814a210338c5

~~~~
# Vendored wasi-libc

`include/` and `lib/libc.a` in this directory are vendored from the
wasi-sdk-24 release (`wasi-sysroot-24.0.tar.gz`,
sha256 `35172f7d2799485b15a46b1d87f50a585d915ec662080f005d99153a50888f08`)
by `scripts/vendor-wasm-libc.sh`. Do not hand-edit them; re-run the script.

- `lib/libc.a`: the wasm32-wasi build of wasi-libc, unmodified.
- `include/`: the subset of wasi-libc's public headers that quickjs's C
  sources need to compile against `libc.a` for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`.
  `wasi/api.h` carries one commented-out `#error` (see the patch step in
  `vendor-wasm-libc.sh`) so it can be included outside an actual wasi
  target; the rest are otherwise unmodified aside from the provenance
  comment the script prepends to each file.

wasi-libc is licensed MIT / Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception / Apache-2.0
(https://github.com/WebAssembly/wasi-libc/blob/main/LICENSE); wasi-sdk,
which builds and publishes this compiled artifact, is Apache-2.0 WITH
LLVM-exception (https://github.com/WebAssembly/wasi-sdk/blob/main/LICENSE).
Both are permissive with no copyleft terms; only `libc.a`'s object code and
the headers above are vendored here, not wasi-sdk's clang/LLVM toolchain.

To update: bump `WASI_SDK_VERSION_MAJOR`/`MINOR` and `WASI_SYSROOT_SHA256`
at the top of `vendor-wasm-libc.sh` (the checksum is published on the
release page, https://github.com/WebAssembly/wasi-sdk/releases/tag/wasi-sdk-<major>),
then re-run `sh scripts/vendor-wasm-libc.sh` from the repo root.
~~~~

### Text 4dd596e085d863ce

~~~~
escape-simd licensing and attribution
====================================

Original contributions to this project are licensed under the MIT license in
LICENSE. Portions derived from sonic-rs and sonic-simd remain licensed under
Apache-2.0, reproduced in LICENSE-APACHE. The MIT license does not replace the
Apache-2.0 terms for those portions. The escape-simd package therefore declares
"MIT AND Apache-2.0", not a choice between the two licenses.

The json-escape-simd and html-escape-simd facade source files are MIT-licensed.
Their escape-simd dependency contains the Apache-2.0 portions described below;
the license texts and this notice accompany all three packages.

sonic-rs and sonic-simd
----------------------

Upstream: https://github.com/cloudwego/sonic-rs
Reference: v0.5.5, resolving to commit
41ae6e8a5962da26eab34432d1412f35ea6d199e
Upstream package authors: Volo Team <volo@cloudwego.io>

Immutable source and license references:
https://github.com/cloudwego/sonic-rs/blob/41ae6e8a5962da26eab34432d1412f35ea6d199e/src/util/string.rs
https://github.com/cloudwego/sonic-rs/tree/41ae6e8a5962da26eab34432d1412f35ea6d199e/sonic-simd/src
https://github.com/cloudwego/sonic-rs/blob/41ae6e8a5962da26eab34432d1412f35ea6d199e/LICENSE
https://github.com/cloudwego/sonic-rs/blob/41ae6e8a5962da26eab34432d1412f35ea6d199e/sonic-simd/LICENSE

The upstream tree at this commit has no NOTICE file. This is an attribution
and modification notice added by escape-simd, not an upstream NOTICE file.
The cited source files have no individual copyright notices; no upstream
copyright owner or year is inferred here.

In the escape-simd crate (crates/escape-simd in the repository):
- src/lib.rs contains the JSON escape tables from src/util/string.rs.
- src/simd/util.rs adapts escape_unchecked and check_cross_page from that file.
- The format_string kernels in src/simd/{avx2,avx512,neon,sse2,v128}.rs adapt
  its string-escaping algorithm.
- src/simd/{bits,traits,avx2,neon,sse2,v128}.rs adapt the corresponding
  sonic-simd modules; src/simd/mod.rs adapts sonic-simd/src/lib.rs.
  The ARM bitmask references in bits.rs and neon.rs are retained in the source.

These files were modified by escape-simd contributors: the escaping routines
were extracted from the JSON parser, SIMD selection was changed to runtime
dispatch, the SIMD interface was reduced, and architecture-specific kernels
were split and optimized (including AVX-512). Buffer handling and explicit
unsafe blocks were adapted, endian handling was corrected, and shared kernels
were extended with feature-gated HTML escaping. File headers identify the
relevant source and changes. These are modified derivatives, not unmodified
copies of the upstream files.

json-escape-simd 3.0.2
---------------------

The same upstream attribution applies to the borrowed portions in src/lib.rs
and src/simd/*.rs of json-escape-simd 3.0.2, before the workspace split:
https://github.com/napi-rs/escape-simd/tree/a7eb4e70c5dc007ec618c53914e3301a0c159af7

The original 3.0.2 crate archive has SHA256
35e770254dd7802184595b1d30da2a15cb72569e2aca2b177aef8d22eac8a693.
Its manifests declare MIT, but its 15 files omit the license texts and notices.
These files supply the missing notices; they do not change that already
published archive or relicense the upstream portions.
~~~~

### Text 7f976f7e9cb2d87d

~~~~
# License

The licensing of these crates is a bit complicated:
- The crates `objc2`, `block2`, `objc2-foundation` and `objc2-encode` are
  [currently][#23] licensed under [the MIT license][MIT].
- All other crates are trio-licensed under the [Zlib], [Apache-2.0] or [MIT]
  license, at your option.

Furthermore, the crates are (usually automatically) derived from Apple SDKs,
and that may have implications for licensing, see below for details.

[#23]: https://github.com/madsmtm/objc2/issues/23
[MIT]: https://opensource.org/license/MIT
[Zlib]: https://zlib.net/zlib_license.html
[Apache-2.0]: https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0


## Apple SDKs

These crates are derived from Apple SDKs shipped with Xcode. You can obtain a
copy of the Xcode license at:

https://www.apple.com/legal/sla/docs/xcode.pdf

Or by typing `xcodebuild -license` in your terminal.

From reading the license, it is unclear whether distributing derived works
such as these crates are allowed?

But in any case, to practically use these crates, you will have to link, and
that only works when you have the correct Xcode SDK available to provide the
required `.tbd` files, which is why we choose to still use the normal SPDX
identifiers in the crates (Xcode is required to use the crates, and when using
Xcode you have already agreed to the Xcode license).
~~~~

files. See LICENSE-other-bits for the text of that license.
